This causes a split in the human psyche between living unconsciously(like non human animals) and living consciously. This causes anxiety because suddenly now you are aware of your own mortality. Non human animals don't have a sense of 'self' and live ephemerally, moment to moment unlike humans who have developed consciousness and are now able to retain memories and know they are going to die. This develops the fiction of 'self'. They are no longer able to perceive themselves as one with the universe and view themselves as separate from it. Non human animals dont have this mental theatre inside themselves. The moment they have any physiological impulse, they discharge it.   
>   
> Therefore this existential anxeity naturally drives humans to religion. Primitive religions were shamanistic among the hunter gatherers. Why would they be different if the cause of the problem is the same amongst everybody? This is where the material reality of the outside world comes in. Tribes in different geographical locations came up with different types of religions. The material reality outside them was so varied. Some places were cold, some were hot, some places were highly forested, some places were an ocean of grassland. You get the point. This caused different types of religions. But the material reality of the psyche is the same amongst all. The material reality of the human brain makes us predisposed to religion and spirituality. But here is the kicker. > @anthraxman Marx never said revolution was inevitable. He explicitly says people make their own history. They just don't do it as they please. They have to grapple with the material conditions specific to the moment of history they exist in. If Marx's materialism were mechanical and deterministic, his call to the working class to organise, unite and rise up would be him needlessly making a fuss, because history unravels on its set path either way. Marx clearly doesn't believe this. He calls the proletariat the subject of history because people do have agency and while material conditions set the stage for revolution by pushing people into a state of depravity that makes them rise up against their subjugation, actively organising as a class and developing class consciousness is still required to effect the sublation of capitalism.

> yes, and this is why neoliberalism has no answer to marx. the egalitarian culture that neoliberal societies push while playing favorites with businesses gives the people an incentive to want a total destruction of the system and redistribution of wealth. this, if achieved, sets precedent for a lot of very unjust, self-impoverishing, and aggressive policy. the revolutionaries thought that running a state like robin hood would bring justice, yet all it did in the end was make them the new aggressor.   
>   
> marx contradicted himself by working backwards, staying just close enough to those egalitarian ideals to drag economics into the mix, but missing the mark entirely when it came to making a just economic framework; his focus on collectives and positive liberties made a dysfunctional system that could both excuse mass violence and slavery through guilt and necessity. he failed to understand capitalism, and his influence led many to blame mixed-market economies for their woes and label them as capitalist economies when they clearly weren't. the main reason these mixed-market economies thrived in the face of communism was because they demanded less of people, despite often still demanding too much in exchange for too little.  
>   
> the real solution to neoliberalism is not to steal from the rich and give to the poor, but to limit the state's authority to a point where they cannot interfere with the economy unless it is to stop aggressive practices (or better yet, aggression would be stopped by the people instead, for boycotting would be more effective without a state subsidizing a business or protecting its intellectual property; the market would decide such fates), keeping the market free. most big businesses today would fail if they had no backing by the state, and we should let them fail so that our local businesses can prosper and be held to the standards of the communities wherein they exist. there is no contradiction of having a truly free and competitive market alongside respect for individual rights. the free market *is* the respect of your individual rights.

The comment section is absolutely a reflection of how his videos blew up. People might find he had some wrong claims (I mean hell this is just a lecture to high school students), but he is absolutely a master at storytelling as a way to teach and get across ideas. This is a skill that's been missed in the majority of teachers today. In fact, when I went to the University of Toronto for my bachelor's, they had the audacity to have professors who could barely speak English who had some lectures on their own papers that screamed irrelevancy to the main topic they were supposed to teach. At no point does this professor claim he has all the facts too. He might very well misinterpret and be wrong about a few things, but if you knew nothing about Marx at all, this video alone will generate your curiosity to do your own research on the subject.

### **@limaigorr** | 👍 356 likes

jiang xueqin has much historic knowledge. but compared to his other classes this one is strange. there is theoretical mess. for him, there is explanation not comprehended by material dialectics. this is fundamentally illogical. 

marx developed a logic system, capable of overcoming even his own historical synthesis if proven incorrect through material dialectics, which is not circumscribed to economics materialism. materialism is not circumscribed to how much wealth each people, class or individual has. material refers to everything observed by human thought. it is the overall true historical condition and structure of peoples, not only economics, but also cultural and societal conditions. 
evidentially, religion cannot be excluded by material dialectics on human development. even if marx, on his terms, criticised it, or if communist endeavors criminalised religion practices. this science transcends its creator synthesis and the historical experiments, because its main logic instrument is a self correcting universal system.

material refers to history facts and events, not solely wealth. that is why marxism self presents as an universal framework of thought, beyond any human or historical bias. it is very basic in this sense. 
the thesis professor jiang presents are very commom beliefs among liberal and neoliberal alienated culture. that, in some level, reproduces capitalistic propaganda, that marxism and communism are “bad and evil”, because marx is not very smart and because stalinistic communism is very oppressive, as if politics and history can be successfully observed through biased perspectives, instead of thesis against synthesis.
for example, in communist/socialist experiments, most famines were the result of political and scientific incapabilities, sometimes incompetence, trying to face bourgeoisie war on nations.

this is disorienting and utterly fails to explain the marxist critic. marxism is not about marx and communism is a never yet achieved utopia. marxism is the critic science of history and capitlism, while socialism and communism are the idealised political visualisation of how to build and endeavor a commom society.

Oh really? 10:50
We didn't develop farming because there was too little food. Premeditated inventing was rare. When there was no food we died or migrated or we were stealing from a tribe nearby. We didn't even think about growing grain - as our main source of food was animal... that's also why, just after developing farming, we jumped to herding hahaha 

We developed farming by side effect because hunters-gaterers were meeting in some places to exchange intel and in these places with time some people started staying permanently and this created a new situation, new circumstances and a new process. We baked bread much earlier than we invented farming of grain - we were doing fine with wild grain for very long time and nobody cared about improving it for any reason. The same as we herded very long without anyone coming up with a stupid idea to ride animals...  

And religion was a side effect of a bigger denser group living in one spot without natural human activities blabbing too much, exchanging new stupid ideas too much and asking stupid questions nobody previously needed for anything ;) 

Literacy, language, symbolism first started in Vinca culture in Europe - fishing culture not agrocultural or commercial or even steppe herding culture. It created social stratification and a new social class (with standardized masks, first elite). You could compare it with Romans with their latin, their sacred laws in latin and need to get a permission to learn their language to become part of this exclusive community. Feeling better and striving for becoming even better. Nobility. Nobility was start of civilization. But nobility forgetting their source, duties and social cohesion with world around kills civilization. Overcivilizing savagifies.

Priests are just a personification of part of Patriarch's function. They aren't required at all for any civilization nor codified religion. Custom was enough. Anyone saying: Our custom is not to eat this kind of shellfish!(and btw you die from it) is fulfilling this role.  

Steppe civilizations didn't rely on agriculture nor were created by agriculture. 
Agricultural societies actually force unity, cohesion, mixing, if possible peaceful coegistence, long house mentality, consensus - kumbaya stagnation, stable regulations. Like China.

Feudalism was against any industrial progress. We saw it in Japan. We saw it in Eastern Europe. These were proper conservative societies where there was no pressing need to invent anything. And there was little commerce to build critical mass more making merchant mindset prevailing. 
Even Rome was innovating but not industrial. 

True devouring everything capitalism started on Madera. Before that sustainable ways were enough and had no serious competition. 
Prussia innovated from sense of danger, in defense. Rome did the same - because survival instinct was their funding principle.

I love this channel. It's so refreshing!!!

Thats not to say he couldnt have used the term once in a while for a specific case, he would probably also like Teleonomy better, but dialectic materialism is not fundamentally teleological at all.

>
> ​@Osterochse well, maybe the term can be used but I still find it a stretch. Marx envisioned Communism as the end of human pre-history indeed, but since there is no evidence whatsoever that things will end like that, does that mean that anything that we envision or even predict an end is teleological? Sounds awkward. But my point was that I doubt he thought of "goal" (requires an agency) or purpose (function?) in that regard, I doubt he ever wrote the word. And it has been sort of hijacked by metaphysics anyway, he could maybe said economic history felt teleonomic though, in that case meaning programmed with goal directness, towards Communism, but I dont think he did. And teleonomy was coined in the 50s. Usually when we talk about teleology stretching it away from idealists we usually use it to deal with something we already know the end goal is real. its not just a projection. Like if you say sex drive is teleological, thats ok, because we know how it ends. If you say a turtle goes to the shore in order to lay eggs, thats also teleological, we saw the whole process, though we in biology will rather call it a teleonomy instead, to avoid confusion. My last statement holds firm though, that dialectic materialism on its own is not teleological because it requires constant re-adaptation, its fundamentally bounded to contingency, emergence, complexity, you cannot predict the future via pragmatic dialectic materialism and thats why I dont think Marx really believed in a program theory (determined) for human relations. Unless he did, but loosely, wishfully.

Hegel believed in ideas driving history, while Marx argued that material conditions (economics) shape history.

Hegel's dialectic focused on contradictions in thought, while Marx's dialectic focused on contradictions in class relations.

Hegel saw the state as the realization of moral life, while Marx saw it as a tool of oppression.

Hegel emphasized philosophy and consciousness, whereas Marx emphasized practical action and revolution

First of all, when ever Communism was fully achieved? Never, because Communism is not a state of affair, but it is a process in motion. So let's talk of Socialist countries, i.e. countries led by a Communist Party which have/had some very precise long term (Utopian?) objectives.
Second, when ever Socialist countries were left alone by the capitalist/imperialist core, which had/have the advantage over them to exploit for free (slavery) all the wealth of the third world (Africa, South America, and Middle East), and have the advantage of not being tied by any moral restraint in killing, stealing, cheating, invading other countries? Socialist countries have always been attacked, put in jeopardy, subject to economic sanctions, so their life, in a predominant capitalist world, has, and is up to now, in constant danger of being destroyed by outside.
Third, why do you not consider the historical contexts in which communists revolutions succeeded? They all succeeded in backward countries (Russia), or in countries ruined and exploited by colonialism (China, Cuba, Vietnam). How could you expect those countries, that had still to develop or had lost a well educated ruling class, not to commit mistakes? Even more so because capitalism is like a climbing plant, that is difficult to eradicate, while Communism is like a bonsai, and needs daily, precise care.

I like a lot of your videos, this one is disappointing. I’m all for a thoughtful critique of Marxism but this is more shadow boxing than that. The thesis, antithesis, synthesis understanding of dialectical thinking is a common misunderstanding. Dialectical thinking is understanding how things like nature, technology, etc exist on their own and how they rub against one another to change one another . Theres not a grandiose vision that we are marching into progress automatically. 

More importantly Marx stressed modes of production. Modes of production tells us how we relate to the things we use and the things we create. Why do we create them. It goes deeper than that but really Marx is saying that in order to understand a society you have to understand modes of production. When you understand that you can understand the dominant ideas that result from this. I’ve read the first volume of das kapital, Marx does not reduce human history simply into what motivates human beings. Rather he discusses modes of production which ideology justifies. It literally impacts what we think in a given era.

The material reality of the human psyche is it has developed a mental simulation of the world within itself. Basically it has developed consciousness. And fundamentally the human consciousness is the world of appearance. Its not the thing in itself. This causes a split in the human psyche between living unconsciously(like non human animals) and living consciously. This causes anxiety because suddenly now you are aware of your own mortality. Non human animals don't have a sense of 'self' and live ephemerally, moment to moment unlike humans who have developed consciousness and are now able to retain memories and know they are going to die. This develops the fiction of 'self'. They are no longer able to perceive themselves as one with the universe and view themselves as separate from it. Its kind of obvious now why this would cause immense anxiety. Now you have to contend with your own morality and the meaning of your life. Non human animals dont have this mental theatre inside themselves. The moment they have any physiological impulse, they discharge it.   
>   
> Therefore this existential anxeity naturally drives humans to religion. Primitive religions were shamanistic among the hunter gatherers. These shamans were rare individuals who have achieved the ability to turn off their consciousness and become one with reality itself. But these primitive religions were so varied and different. Why would they be different if the cause of the problem is the same amongst everybody? This is where the material reality of the outside world comes in. Tribes in different geographical locations came up with different types of religions. The material reality outside them was so varied. Some places were cold, some were hot, some places were highly forested, some places were an ocean of grassland. You get the point. This caused different types of religions. But the material reality of the psyche is the same amongst all. The material reality of the human brain makes us predisposed to religion and spirituality. But here is the kicker. These religions are ultimately still fictions. They were fictions that can only approximate reality. So Humanity is doomed to keep iterating different fictional models of reality but can never accurately describe it. And this is what drives humans history. As technologies progress, we start revising our religions. And communism is the latest iteration of this religious striving.   
>   
> My theory is the primitive religions were much better approximations than the later ones. They were able to incorporate all the physiological drives of the human body more than the later ones. The primitive religions(pagan religions) had gods for lust, love, food. Gods for natural elements like sky, sun, water, etc. Gods for war, underworld, etc. Basic point is they were able to capture the full range of human experience. Later religions vilified many natural impulses like war, lust, etc. Unlike popular marxist myth that hunter gatherer societies were some form of primitive communism, these societies were ones that drew immense pleasure from killing the enemy tribe and to prove the superiority of their own gods. Primitive communism did exist, but only within the tribe, but this does not extend outside the tribe. Now the material reality of why you would want to vanquish the other tribe all boils down to the selfish desire to provide a comfortable life for your progeny. This desire is maybe the one immutable human desire that cannot be done away with because of evolutionary pressures. And this is what communism suppresses. The selfish desire to accumulate resources for your progeny. In modern day, tribes have been replaced by races. The material reality of genetic difference cannot be overcome to form a globalized proletariat class. Race and religious differences are not false consciousness. Its the material reality that communism cannot overcome. The only way for communism to work will be an all out global war among races and religions and ultimately a sole winner, a genetically homogeneous  group that can inherit this world and implement communism. Immigration is now the single major issue in Europe and America. And it's only going to get worse notwithstanding all the secular people who don't see 'race'. In times of crises one will stick with their own race even if they are extremely liberal and rational. Ultimately you don't want anything happening to your extended family and interpolating that, your race. This is why despite the varied political opinions of Israelis, most of them still don't care about what's happening to the Palestinians. Even if rationally you know what's going on is wrong, you cannot empathize with their plight on a human level because you know they harbour ill intent(justified or unjustified) toward you. They know that in the hypothetical situation of roles being reversed and if the Palestinians had superior technological and military might, they would not take the high road and would inflict the same suffering on them.

### **@MartinThau** | 👍 3 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgxPfwdoKWwVSDHcCNp4AaABAg)

Alfred Sohn-Rethel, inspired by reading Marx’s Capital at age 16, developed a radical idea: our very way of thinking—our logic, science, and abstract concepts—stems from the act of commodity exchange. Unlike Marx, who focused on the material relations of production, Sohn-Rethel traced the origins of abstraction to the social form of exchange itself. He argued that when we trade goods, we artificially equate unlike things (bread, shoes, etc.) through money. This act of making things equal—exchange abstraction—does not remain outside us but shapes the very structure of thought: the mind learns to think in terms of identity, quantity, and equivalence. Money is thus not just an external tool but an inner coin that teaches us to categorize the world in numerical and comparable terms. Sohn-Rethel’s key correction to Marx lies in showing how economic forms (like money) give rise to cognitive forms (like logic). This socio-genetic epistemology reveals that what we call rational thought is historically rooted in the practice of market exchange. To change thought, then, we must also change how we act economically.

### **@nme-bp7cf** | 👍 62 likes

as much as I take your criticisms of Marx with respect to the historical foundations of some of his claims, I don't think he was fundamentally wrong when it comes to materialism. Religion cannot be more important than material wealth because if one's life span is only 10 years (health problems, etc), you have a completely different outlook and a different capacity to even hypothesize and connect with a so-called spiritual world. I think the faith aspect comes after capitalism. I think the reformation allowed for a rethinking about civilization, but it wasn't because of some new anxiety as a result of theological developments. I don't agree with that. I need more concrete evidence. If Kant is right, and our imagination of the physical world is the fundamental truth as we know it, then Marx is also correct in saying that our imagination of the physical world is contingent on how much of it we can control. And that is dependant on power and wealth to a large extent. And should we ignore all the labour struggles in France, Germany, United States, and Britain? Did the disenfranchised just sit there? Did those at the top of the hierarchy just sit there? This idea that we must focus on ourselves and connect with our inner-spirituality irrespective of our position in a  social hierarchy is flawed and quite frankly stupid and egoistic, in my respectful opinion. Of course asceticism is real and a meaningful practice, philosophy and religion (elements of Buddhism and Hinduism), but that does not justify a system of social relations which is predicated on the wealth of some over the many. Religion should not be used to justify such a system. One can reconcile communism with spirituality. But one cannot reconcile spirituality with a fundamentally oppressive system of social relations. Also this was one of the best videos I've ever watched in my entire life. I've attended Western Univesitiy in Canada, studying sociology and this was hands down the  most stimulating and useful explanation of marxism I've ever come across, better than all of my university education put together. Thank you so much for this, I hope one day that I can meet you and have a discussion.

>
> @CovfefeBaileys  @nme-bp7cf First you invoke a materialist explanation for emotion in social relations - "We have internalized the logic of capitalism so we are "forced" to compete with eachother which drives resentment, jealousy, etc" - and then your solution is to negate them through introspection and spiritual revolution through the Buddhist practice of removal - "So we have to start with the removal (buddhism) or integration (Jung) of the self in order to effectuate change in society". This is fetishism in action. As the other user said, religion as the engine of history is wrong. You cannot reason people on the whole into ego-death because the very existence of capitalism generates and compels human beings via incentive-structures to internalise the profit-seeking logic of the firm as a starting point for their social activity (fetishised as legal 'subjectivity'). This structural necessity will always override "spiritual insights" (no matter how correct they are about the illusion of the 'self') in the long term because ideas circulate and proliferate within the context of emergent properties and self-reproducing systems. The latter have macro-scale laws of motion not reducible to their parts that structure and shape said parts. The only way to end this structural tendency to fetishise and reify the self is to abolish the conditions that reproduce it (which you hint at implicitly in saying economic agents are '"forced" to compete') by grasping the immanent necessity of the abolition of the law of value.  
>   
> This puts us at a problem, because how can human beings change this state of affairs if the individual is compelled to produce certain outcomes in the long run? As Hegel said "freedom is the appreciation of necessity". The idealist looks inward and finds self-transformation as the independent variable, a lived lifestyle to be adopted and replicated as social contagion. The dialectical materialist looks at the social contradictions driving change at the macro-level and analyzes the forces that imprint themselves on individuals as relations and manifestations of those contradictions. In other words, identifying the conflicting forces that allow for the idea that someone needs to change themselves or the system to emerge and be internalised in the first place. The various ideas, moral theories and unconscious desires of individuals all have social origin in this process. This is basic materialism, but the point is the mechanism of change. Marxism doesn't offer a deterministic, fully formed proletariat ready to take control as soon as the correct idea circulates, but instead a dialectical understanding of the relation of micro to macro that provides the basis for an actual, scientific mechanism of change. Marx's studies identify a particular relation with no vested interest in the system that can socially reproduce itself without relying on other classes. The fusion of the spontaneous practice of this class with the scientific discovery of surplus value and the immanent laws of motion of capital that arise from its production is the basis of a real cognitive map of capitalist totality. This is the foundation of Marxist dialectical pedagogy. The result of internalizing this map is a call to action that it is both desirable and necessary to unite active advanced elements of a disparate, disorganized class with advanced theory and to raise the consciousness of the intermediate elements through revolutionary transformation of social relations. A structural potential negation becomes actualised through the mediation of social activity via  (proletarian vanguard).   
>   
> "Why was Karl Marx -- who believed that industrial capitalism would evolve into a worker's paradise -- wrong? " this is a statement from the description. The self-evidence of the failure of communism is dependant on common sense, the kind of empiricist historicism that Althusser outlined and people use freely to make bold claims about Marxism in religious or bourgeois interests. But that doesn't make it true. We can still compare the results of Marxist method to its competition - including "spiritual transformation" and any of Bukanin's, Landheimer or Kropotkin's pre-scientific ideas. The mere fact that the macro-dynamics of a third of the world were - for at least 50 years - structured and economically developed without the predominant operation of the law of value is remarkable. There is simply no achievement of human collective agency against subordination to alien social forces comparable in class history. Let's start with that and how a theoretical system that could produce such an achievement was able conceptualize it's own defeat (hint: Marxism-Leninism-Maoism has a far more scientific evaluation of the origins and reproduction of capitalist restoration and how to solve it than vulgar, functionalist theories of "bureaucratic elites" forwarded by petty-bourgeois professors).

> While his understanding of Marx is not accurate, his rebuttal of the Marxist theory of historical materialism is correct. He didn't elucidate the critique really well. My understanding is the marxist form of materialism is focuses more on the world outside of oneself. It underestimates the material reality within humans themselves. The material reality of the human psyche is it has developed a mental simulation of the world within itself. Basically it has developed consciousness. And fundamentally the human consciousness is the world of appearance. Its not the thing in itself. This causes a split in the human psyche between living unconsciously(like non human animals) and living consciously. This causes anxiety because suddenly now you are aware of your own mortality. Non human animals don't have a sense of 'self' and live ephemerally, moment to moment unlike humans who have developed consciousness and are now able to retain memories and know they are going to die. This develops the fiction of 'self'. They are no longer able to perceive themselves as one with the universe and view themselves as separate from it. Its kind of obvious now why this would cause immense anxiety. Now you have to contend with your own morality and the meaning of your life. Non human animals dont have this mental theatre inside themselves. The moment they have any physiological impulse, they discharge it.   
>   
> Therefore this existential anxeity naturally drives humans to religion. Primitive religions were shamanistic among the hunter gatherers. These shamans were rare individuals who have achieved the ability to turn off their consciousness and become one with reality itself. But these primitive religions were so varied and different. Why would they be different if the cause of the problem is the same amongst everybody? This is where the material reality of the outside world comes in. Tribes in different geographical locations came up with different types of religions. The material reality outside them was so varied. Some places were cold, some were hot, some places were highly forested, some places were an ocean of grassland. You get the point. This caused different types of religions. But the material reality of the psyche is the same amongst all. The material reality of the human brain makes us predisposed to religion and spirituality. But here is the kicker. These religions are ultimately still fictions. They were fictions that can only approximate reality. So Humanity is doomed to keep iterating different fictional models of reality but can never accurately describe it. And this is what drives humans history. As technologies progress, we start revising our religions. And communism is the latest iteration of this religious striving.   
>   
> My theory is the primitive religions were much better approximations than the later ones. They were able to incorporate all the physiological drives of the human body more than the later ones. The primitive religions(pagan religions) had gods for lust, love, food. Gods for natural elements like sky, sun, water, etc. Gods for war, underworld, etc. Basic point is they were able to capture the full range of human experience. Later religions vilified many natural impulses like war, lust, etc. Unlike popular marxist myth that hunter gatherer societies were some form of primitive communism, these societies were ones that drew immense pleasure from killing the enemy tribe and to prove the superiority of their own gods. Primitive communism did exist, but only within the tribe, but this does not extend outside the tribe. Now the material reality of why you would want to vanquish the other tribe all boils down to the selfish desire to provide a comfortable life for your progeny. This desire is maybe the one immutable human desire that cannot be done away with because of evolutionary pressures. And this is what communism suppresses. The selfish desire to accumulate resources for your progeny. In modern day, tribes have been replaced by races. The material reality of genetic difference cannot be overcome to form a globalized proletariat class. Race and religious differences are not false consciousness. Its the material reality that communism cannot overcome. The only way for communism to work will be an all out global war among races and religions and ultimately a sole winner, a genetically homogeneous  group that can inherit this world and implement communism. Sort of what Eren from Attack on Titan had in mind. And this is becoming increasingly obvious in our modern experiments of multiculturalism in the western world. Immigration is now the single major issue in Europe and America. And it's only going to get worse notwithstanding all the secular people who don't see 'race'. In times of crises one will stick with their own race even if they are extremely liberal and rational. Ultimately you don't want anything happening to your extended family and interpolating that, your race. This is why despite the varied political opinions of Israelis, most of them still don't care about what's happening to the Palestinians. Even if rationally you know what's going on is wrong, you cannot empathize with their plight on a human level because you know they harbour ill intent(justified or unjustified) toward you. They know that in the hypothetical situation of roles being reversed and if the Palestinians had superior technological and military might, they would not take the high road and would inflict the same suffering on them.   
>   
> Sorry for rambling on and going on multiple side tangents. My basic point is Marxism is lousy materialism. The meme 'Muh human nature' is infact true and valid criticism of communism.

Wow, what a wild thesis this lecture is based on.
I wonder if I should be surprised how few antithesis (and correction of "facts") are in the comments.

11:03 quote: "There is not enoug food to feed everyone" ... wait what.
At a time when there was a world wide population of less than 10 million people and in fertile areas where people surely also found a lot of animals to hunt (like in the Levant region about 10 k years ago).
Someone has to point out to me when and where Karl Marx maid this teleological claim.
But let's put that beside for now.

Even more "interesting" is the thesis/claim that humans are looking for religion or rather a god or want to be near god rather than having enough ressources to survive and maybe a roof over the head.
First of all ... what god are we talking about that all humans want to be close to?
When did humans start to feel this need? Not before we switched from Polytheism to Monotheism but after paganism?
Where are the facts to underline the thesis that humans want religion or to be close to god?
Or does Xueqin Jiang mean here the "spirit" Hegel is talking about, which is already in us when he says "god"?

By the way, a single event of a suizidal child is tragic but doesn't underline any arguement at all!

Communism doesn't fail because of "material" and because it is the same as capitalism.
It always failed because of authoritarianism. The same is true for capitalism (like the so called 3. empire).
Aswell as feudelism. Humans don't stand up and risk their lifes by fighting those isms because a lack of religion or a lack of access to a god but instead of lack of equality and/or food.

It is a pitty that I can't find any convincing arguement in this lecture.
Is communism the answer, a good solution? I don't know. Humans never really tried it without it ending in authoritarianism and surpression (as far as I know).
But I doubt that we as humans are even able to. The authority seem to be part of us, maybe out of natural necessity. Be it as the head of a family or even of a community. Some have the "gene" to lead and (too) many relate on it to survive long enough befor they finally become head of a family themself. To bad that this authoritarion behaviour often/mostly ends up in surpression and even worse instead of just being used for protecting and teaching.
Welcome in the animal world.

### **@DracoHouston-kl6cq** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=Ugz3qDOz9S2FiDD2rht4AaABAg)

Reading through the comments, I am glad I decided not to skip to this video and judge his argument solely on my own reading of Marx. While there is a bunch I would probably have quibbled with had I been in the room, there is a lot of food for thought in these lectures. The overall claim (roughly paraphrasing) that Superstructure determines what you can even imagine you will do with the Base is bold, but fascinating. The videos leading up to the modern ages set up the argument, then the history of the modern ages, including our present, lets him illustrate the point. Mainly, our materialistic and utilitarian society is not that imaginative. To the point where the ideas that might make some truly transformative technology possible (eg nano-tech, AGI, robot butlers) might never come out of the capitalist system, ushering in communism. Because we don't make people like Kant, Hegel, or Marx, anymore. 

I feel like this is an argument only a professor of literature could make, and it took you over 60 hours to make it, via high school level history lessons, but it gave me a lot to think about. So thank you for making it. I will try to synthesize something out of it. I am not ready to discard the importance of material conditions to history, but I will try to accept that beliefs matter more than they are usually judged in analysis of events, historical and current. Generally, we don't really believe that people can truly believe them. It isn't rational. And yet, they're moved.

### **@gebrehiwotewnetu358** | 👍 36 likes

This was not an simply an oversimplification of Marx. It was an intentional trivialization. 
  People who read this comment, please read Marx for yourselves, or the Prof. Prabhat Patnaik readers on all the Capitals.
   The underlying thesis of Marx is not that people want to 'feel as part of a bigger mission'. 
He is a materialist. doesn't really go into 'feelings that much'.
  
Because that Lawyer does not produce any of the commodities he consumes - he relies on sets of social rules (laws) that are designed to ensure he gets a share of the produced goods of that society (for example food, through a medium of exchange money), which is why he maintains the system out of self interest, because he is from the class of people who study law and eat farmed food, not farm the land and obey the law.

But the farmers only till the land and keep this lawyer fed for free because of the social rules that constrain them to participate in that society, that social constraint is common interest in public goods (e.g., money and policing of trade routes) and common protection from violence, which is interpreted and managed through social processes that we call  politics. 

It is in the interests of the people who produce the goods to have more of a say on the rules and the money and the policing through politics so that they get a fare share of the resources, as the Lawyer has a disproportionate say on the social rules that govern that distribution process, and lawyers tend to hold their efforts as more valuable than everyone else's and definitely they undervalue the physical labor that feeds and clothes them.

People need to have a control over the means of production that produce the car, the money, the house, the food, because without it you will be impoverished when the time comes. Those who have the power over the means of production will cut you out of the share of the common resources when they find it politically convenient to do so.

### **@tampa1342**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgyLcURlOn0waJMU9094AaABAg)

Another very interesting lecture, and really very logically elaborated. Personally, I believe that both socio-political systems, capitalism and communism, have their fair share of negative aspects. It depends on society's perception of which of the two systems has more flaws. Unfortunately, the entire world is based on who can create more capital, and governments operate on this principle, showcasing their power either through military strength or economic might. Ultimately, it all boils down to some form of power.

There are people for whom material wealth is a priority, while on the other side, there are those for whom spirituality takes precedence. This balance of priorities has never really been present, not even in the very beginnings of civilization. Religion or spirituality tends to unite people more strongly than economic or class conditions; I believe this is largely because spiritual solidarity is easier to achieve than economic solidarity.

If it were economically possible to achieve a system where the representatives of authority are not as financially powerful as a select few, and if everyone could create as much as they deem necessary, it would approach something almost ideal. Totalitarian states in which authorities present themselves to the public as if they were gods represent a completely distorted and abusive perception of solidarity and justice. Yes, Karl Marx had good intentions, but he misunderstood many things and built a quasi-solution on those foundations. Nevertheless, I will always choose the absolute freedom of every individual, no matter how many possible class differences there may be. Additionally, one cannot live solely on spirituality in this world, which does not mean that the foremost quality of a person should not be based on nobility and the purity of heart and mind. I really love this approach to lectures, which is based on established facts and encourages independent critical thinking. It does not brainwash like most of today’s schools and universities, which are almost 90% based on lies. Thank you prof. Jiang.

### **@AnCapJohn** | 👍 5 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgxgMYAT13UEDrondGd4AaABAg)

Karl Marx’s theories are fundamentally flawed.. His labor theory of value, tying a commodity’s worth to labor alone, is debunked by subjective preferences and marginal utility determine market prices. This error fuels Marx’s surplus value concept, wrongly claiming capitalists steal from workers, when profits stem from entrepreneurial risk and voluntary exchange. Marx’s push for central planning ignores Mises’ economic calculation problem, where the absence of market prices cripples resource allocation, and Hayek’s knowledge problem, where dispersed knowledge defies central coordination, causing stagnation. His class struggle oversimplifies economics, neglecting entrepreneurship and capital, while his deterministic history dismisses individual agency, clashing with individualism. These flaws expose Marx’s ideas as utopian, not scientific, rendering his predictions of capitalism’s collapse theoretically unsound and empirically refuted.

I agree with a few things here, but ultimately capitalism and communism pivot upon who owns the means of production.

Sure, it ties the philosophy to economics and each are a religion of sorts, but the core disagreement between the two revolves around the amount of democracy in the workplace.

Marx believed that history is a spiraling process "History repeats itself, but the second time as a farce"

And in the Communist Manifesto he leaves open the possibility of mutual destruction of all classes/downward movement of the spiral schematically

"The materialist doctrine concerning the changing of circumstances and upbringing forgets that circumstances are changed by men and that it is essential to educate the educator himself. This doctrine must, therefore, divide society into two parts, one of which is superior to society.



The coincidence of the changing of circumstances and of human activity or self-changing can be conceived and rationally understood only as revolutionary practice."

If Marx believed that the class struggle was everything, he would not have been wasting for 20 years for writing "Capital" and identifying the basic laws of capitalism.
The basic laws of capitalism still were and are in work in Soviet Union and China for example like the imperialism

Basically what Marx did not "calculate" is the power of petty -bourgeois movements, the media, the propaganda in the era of monopoly capitalism, and in all of them that also the lack of classical education gives rise to useful idiots.The bureaucraticization of revolutions I don't think you would shock him so much.

### **@Jostradamus2033**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgztwZYnqF_JmtQT16B4AaABAg)

A few notes of my own so far. 
A.) Hunter gatherers may have been taller on average due to selection pressure. Were there more farmers than hunter gatherers and did farmers have a lower mortality rate year over year?.

B.) It seems that status creates a powerful response to incentive structures so a better set of incentive structures could create a society where people compete to create positive outcomes (however implausible this may be it is my takeaway)

C.) The analysis that narrative and main characters (originally religion and god, But I believe this concept applies far more broadly.)  Are the bringers of change as opposed to material conditions and technological progress strictly does hold very true. To ascribe powerful emotional movements with prominent figureheads the role of civilizational change is an idea I find difficult to counterargue.
Addendum: People at least in the united states rarely care about policy rather than the narrative and characters behind it. Politics is about progress and achievement of goals by narrative that society favors. A more favorable and more compelling narrative will win out over a weak or nonexistent narrative even if that side's policy is astronomically better. Albeit the united states more or less has a two-headed monoparty the 2024 election was won by strength of narrative not strength of policy.

### **@chazzismo2768**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgwnNFF08EYDIGqBffJ4AaABAg)

Societies in marxism includes both the (primary) economic base & the social-cultural superstructure. The superstructure includes the influence of religion. But the manifestations of religion primarily depend on the former, despite some give and tug. Look how Christianity has developed uniquely in the USA for instance:

 In the US you have prosperity gospel, evangelism, mormonism, etc as dominant and often uniquely strong trends. These are trends which explicitly build on the uniquely capitalist and settler context of the US (mormonism is literally a settler religion that explains the role of north america in christianity!). As time goes on you also get religions like scientology that are based on distinctly modern american elements: pulp science fiction, hollywood celebrity endorsements, and cults that prey on the isolated and alienated individuals of late capitalism. 

Of course, you can say these influence reality. Conservative right wingers use religious arguments to empower themselves and reshape the economy further to their benefit. Mormons and other missionaries export their beliefs abroad. However, these are extensions of a capitalism and imperialism that already existed and gave birth to them in their unique historical forms. Cultural aspects like religion are like the language / words of history, but the material foundation is the content of what is being said, regardless of language. 

Errors taken by communist states & parties in the past don't necessarily disprove the basic tools of historical materialism applied to a class / material basis. 
At the most you can see that given their place in space and time, they didn't have access to the information that would have allowed them to use this framework effectively. 

Marx's analysis of world politics was limited by his experience leading to false predictions of the origin of the first proletarian revolutions. Thinker-doers like Lenin and Mao subsequently were able to use these tools more effectively and helped to foment actual revolutions , probably because of their positions nearer or outside of the borders of imperialism. Ironically this is a great example of materialism at work: more accurate theory and political practice was produced by those who with a different material context! Of course, they also made mistakes, just like the next generation of revolutionaries will also do. But until the historical materialist method is actually proven false, there is no reason to discard it.

### **@isaidpianissimo2475**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=Ugwg0JQAkg-AWiQtluZ4AaABAg)

After reading hundreds of comments below this video, my major concern is that not very many people have read the original source (or read it carefully enough); here I'm referring to "Capital" or "Das Kapital." In Jiang's lecture he describes 4 subsets of a kind of alienation. Jiang attributes these 4 to Marx. Anyone who's been given something other than a strawman version of Marx (and/or anyone who's actually studied Marx in good faith, and/or been taught by teachers who had read "Capital," then taught from it in good faith) knows that there are different ways to look at alienation. The way Jiang tells us how Marx looked at alienation is wrong. Those in psychology (among other fields) look at alienation the way Jiang (incorrectly) attributed to Marx. Marx had a completely different way to say how some people are "alienated" from, like, whatever these people are alienated from. I know my last sentence seems a bit vague, but I'm being vague on purpose because I don't want to give away the answer that took me a few weeks in school to acquire, namely, reading "Capital," which is 1,152 pages. (The edition published in 1992, by Random House, is eleven hundred fifty-two pages.)



TL;DR If the above is a li'l too much for you, you're in the wrong business.

Both adamantly preached against self-unaliving, and sample size is in the hundreds of thousands of self-unalivers, ergo, sample sizes are easily large enough. My idea is this: perhaps these religious Germans were so concerned with efficiency that if a brother or a son wasn't producing enough, he was shamed so much that he finally ended himself.

I've been watching lots PH videos recently after having been exposed to a few clips. I must admit that I had been somewhat impressed with some of his perspectives, while others fell a little short, but I didn't think much of it till I watched this video. Unfortunately his "capitalism and communism are the same religion" and "it's religion not the economy" hot takes are just galaxy brained contrarian distinctions without a difference masquerading as profound dichotomies discovered by the keen observation of a wise sage. Suddenly all the questions and doubts that had accumulated working through his video back catalogue made perfect sense, he had a fortuitous 'broken clock' moment at the right time to leverage it on social media, but has failed to repeat it ever since. 
NGL, I'm kind of glad I watched this video when I did, because it's spared me having to litigate all the subtle anti migrant, thinly veiled antisemitism and liberal pro capitalist, "great man theory", "great power politics"  Nietzschean ubermensch bs he front loads his sermons with. The future does not have to echo the past, and just because a thing has not happened before does not mean it won't.
In short, all his post hoc rationalisations and unfounded leaps to conclusion demand the kind of suspension of disbelief I'm just not accustomed to. 
I just can't squint that hard, nor do I want to just to believe a "cool story bro".

lol

### **@TEGRIDY14**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgwDrr0D5q1MoDnaXXZ4AaABAg)

I believe that most humans are capable of love and caring for each other. But I also believe we’re inherently self interested. As societies grow larger, inevitably there will be bad actors who will lurk in the shadows and corrupt any system that’s created.

I believe that creating laws and institutions that incentivize people to be productive and not harmful to each other, is the best we can do. Communism creates a society with no incentives, except to attain power through corruption.

I’m against most socialized benefits. Not because I don’t want everyone’s needs to be met. But because most that I’ve seen have bad incentive structures in my opinion. 

In mass, I believe that people need to feel the cost of not being productive in order for them to be productive.

Perhaps if we have robots to produce resources and build things for us we could rely on socialized benefits. But I don’t know what people would think of their lives, when they are not responsible for their own wellbeing.

I'm yet to watch the lecture, but what the main critique from the comments seem to get forget is that religiousities (astrology, funeral rites, animism, initiation rites, ancestor worship) are far, far, far older than sedentarism or any capacity of surplus production for a priest class. What i get from Marx (and marxists) is that they derive their criticism of religion from a very christian, monotheistic, well defined class reality (confucianism), which is the same you get from the every european thinker post Reinassence.
Materialism seems to fall short once archaeologists expose the human behavior in the pre-historic or very ancient world, or when anthropologists unveil the customs of barely contacted societies on the fringes of the Earth. These people lived and live very spiritual realities, where spirits are everywhere and in contact with men, guiding or cursing their lives,. I think it's a very fair, very useful and very well made criticism of capitalism, but also seems very far from being the all-definer of all human reality, that will be a mystery forever.

### **@LightLeaks-f6s**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgypWvcVyXIvCcwVBld4AaABAg)

To be fair to Marx, his vision was a post-industrial society morphing to communism, not a sudden switch to it from a rural and largely unspohisticated agricultural/ resource rich hinterland like Russia.

He also didn't forsee how the capitalists would be so terrified of communism that they allowed the peasants under their own lash a lot more freedom than they would otherwise have extended to them - hence the ramping up of the evils of capitalism since the threat of communism diminished after the Berlin wall fell.

The eastern bloc was a prophylactic to the excesses of capitalism - and with that no longer in place now the uber-capitalist monopoly seekers' ambition is to own and control everything, with democracy as a facade to keep the peasants placated, docile and obedient.

You see this everywhere - people who in the UK who think voting for Corbyn will fix everything, for example.  Even if there was a landslide for Corbyn, there'd be a military coup within months of his "victory".  You can only have democracy if you vote for the candidates the capitalists who set up the system approve of..

I think that perhaps you risk, as I often do, placing the cart before the horse. 
Suppose the majority of hunter gatherers had, over the millennia, become expert in controlling populations simply by mothers passing to daughters the skill of knowing, by the consistency of vaginal secretions, the exact times of ovulation and therefore were able to control their populations. I understand that this practice can still be found in areas of China for example, but is rare and little heard of until recently in the west.
Jared Diamond suggests that often, rather than necessity being the mother of invention, the opposite applies - invention is the mother of necessity.
Early domestication and invention of items such as the jawbone scythe leads to surplus,
which leads to hierarchy and competition which necessitates increasing populations to, amongst other things, defend against or attack neighbors. Even today, there is a religious emphasis on procreation without contraceptive controls.
It seems to me that the trick was then and remains today to convince those at the bottom of the pyramid that the comparative deprivations that they endure, be they risk of death or the destabilization of their communities for the benefit of those at the top are for their own benefit. Magic and the development of more sophisticated magics that we call religions are simply part of the arsenal of tricks.
I would welcome any critique of the above because, as you often say, it isn't the truth.

### **@syahiidkamil**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgwgiHfSlYBr1nSywxN4AaABAg)

Unpopular fact: Marx's entire materialist turn was basically him rage-quitting idealism after Max Stirner absolutely demolished the Young Hegelians in Der Einzige und sein Eigentum (1844). Stirner called out all their sacred concepts - humanity, society, even Marx's precious "species-being", as nothing but spooks haunting people's minds.

Marx was SO triggered he and Engels wrote like 300+ pages just attacking Stirner in The German Ideology (calling him "Saint Max" sarcastically throughout). The whole "Saint Max" section is basically Marx going "n-no YOU'RE the idealist!" for hundreds of pages while secretly adopting Stirner's critique and just replacing "spooks" with "ideology."

The funniest part? Stirner pointed out that even the proletariat and class consciousness were just new spooks to replace the old religious ones. Marx: "Material conditions determine consciousness!" Also Marx: "Workers of the world unite around this abstract idea of class solidarity!"

Stirner saw it coming, communism would just become another sacred cause, another wheel to crush the unique individual under. And what happened? Exactly what Prof Jiang: describes personality cults, theocracies with communist aesthetics, the unique sacrificed to the collective spook.

For those interested in the transition between hunter-gatherers and agriculturalists, there is a scientific explanation (not religious):
- Human groups did try agriculture and yes, the bad diet caused diseases, short stature and high mortality BUT
- Settling down allowed women to have a baby a year (instead of every 5 years in hunter-gatherers because of the need for the kid to walk)
- All the excess teenage boys was redirected to external aggression (conquest)


So as an ESS (evolutionarily stable strategy) the agriculturalists won. Remember that evolution DOES NOT CARE ABOUT YOU. It's all about the propagation of the genes.
That is why we are now domesticated, obedient slaves.
Religion came AFTER and allowed the culture to explain away (brainwash) the dehumanizing effect of city living.

### **@matthewball9851** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=Ugyw83MpzUzaLcSWpZ14AaABAg)

Marx never claimed that a workers paradise 'was inevitable'. 

Communist manifesto: 

"Freeman and slave, patrician and plebeian, lord and serf, guild-master and journeyman, in a word, oppressor and oppressed, stood in constant opposition to one another, carried on an uninterrupted, now hidden, now open fight, a fight that each time ended, either in a revolutionary reconstitution of society at large, or in the common ruin of the contending classes. "

"common ruin" makes Marx's analysis contingent not prescriptive or inevitable. It means on a long enough time line capitalism produces Communism from its internal contradiction or falls to barbarism and collapse.

I think your interpretation of Hegel here is misguided, you're teaching pop-Hegel, not the actual double negation/Aufhebung that Marx was working from.

### **@pumpkaraoke**

I don't think it's helpful to create a dichotomy between linear progression and cycles. After all, it's Marx who famously said "History repeats itself, first as tragedy, second as farce."  Besides, anyone looking at the world would be hard pressed not to see something like progress (and by this, Marx meant the progression of productivity) given that we all now have hand computers. But you also obviously see repetition, like the class struggles in ancient Greece or ancient Rome, and their counterpart struggles in US slavery. That all being said, this a high school course, and as a high school course, it's excellent, even if it's sometime too fast and too simplistic.

### **@EEggs1**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgwdbydIVS6VZ1smlmB4AaABAg)

I generally really appreciate your content which typically engages well with the dialectic between the material basis of reality and ideological drivers of policy. However, as a Marxist, I think this video is a horrible mischaracterization of Marx. I think on one hand you're engaging with a very surface level reading of Marx at the level of Marxology, rather than engaging with the process of Marxism. I think it's completely inaccurate to say that Marx was unaware of the importance of religion in society. I think you're also just entirely wrong about idealism being the driver of history and not materialism. Religious changes over time are prefigured by underlying changes in the economic structure. As well, you seem hung up on the weird portrayal of Marxism in particular, but communism broadly as "everyone will be equal always". In fact, in Marx's portrayal of communism, he described a world of freedom that allowed people to pursue their own goals and ideals  without having to submit to exploitation under a capitalist's watch. The beginning rhetorical question makes no sense in the context of the 19th century when much of the world was starving. Sure, there was a professional class, a bourgeoisie, and the remains of nobility, but outside of that, many people, including Marx himself, dealt with great food insecurity. The potato famine is actually a perfect example of a political decision, a "social murder" brought about by a) the capitalist drive causing monocrop production in Ireland, and b) the exporting of potatoes out of Ireland for British profits at the same time as they experienced a famine. Norway also suffered a great potato famine, it's why my family moved to America, but the Norwegians suffered significantly less deaths because they weren't simultaneously having the British taking their potatoes while it was going on. Ultimately, if you engage with dialectics, and more broadly with the evolution of Marxism through works of thinkers after he passed, his work takes on far greater analysis than Marx himself produced. And even simply his work, ignoring those who proceeded him, I think you fail to accurately analyze.

### **@amanofnoreputation2164**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgzLPx13bBWnaPNrOAh4AaABAg)

[How do Protestants know if they are favored by God or not?]
The answer they use actually comes from Judaism: Before Christianity and the orthodox church that would go on to become the Catholic church. it was reasoned that if a person had material wealth, _God must have blessed them._ Wealth and favor are taken to be identical and the opposite was also held to be true *quickly leading to many paradoxes and tatutologies.) Nobody could be assumed to be poor for no reason. They must be a bad person and it must be part of God's divine plan that they suffer. 
This doctrine was such that in the book of Job, Job's friends argue that God must have cursed him and took away all his material possessions because he had sinned when, of course, the real reason was that Satan had challenged God to a bet and Job was innocent. This logic is maintained at the end of the story where God restores Job's wealth two-fold. It wouldn't be enough to have rounded out the story with a quick, "Yahweh made an oopsie." Job needed to have his stuff given back in order to _prove_ that he was pious and had been innocent the whole time.  
So this shift to materialism didn't just arbitrarily happen once the Protestant population cast off the papal yoke -- they looked to scripture and imitated the ancient Hebrews. Protestantism was in theis way a kind of Jewish renaissance, just with the continued ostrasization of Jews.

This isn't to say that the oirigin of capitalism is Judaism. The entire point of the Book fo Job is that the Jewish scholarly community was grabbling with this internally and had recognized the flaws in it. As a Jewish reformer, Jesus devoted himself to such contradictions. "Render unto Caeaser," makes it explicit that material wealth is not virtue and is something for the gentiles, not the elect of God. But instead the opposite happened and the Jewish diaspora were forced to become moneylenders.

God is just the idea the holds the community together.

The Soviet Union was state capitalism, not communism,  and China seems to be doing quite well with its market socialism.  There remains the possibility of creating an economic system that will provide other things that humans want beyond material necessities. Capitalism in the West is giving way to rentier financialization and rentier monopolies and to destructive imperialism  for many and neocolonial underdevelopment and perpetual impoverishment for the world's majority. Rentier systems built on unearned passive income are dispossessing of the products of those who labor and are enslaving.  
People want more than religion.  They also want material necessities. freedom from precarity,  freedom from perpetual, unending, and unfulfilling work that allows for creativity and recreation in addition to necessities.

Now I see why this professor is so popular. He appears as a dissident but his purpose is to steer people away from communism and reproduce the status quo. How can he believe that Marx is one of the most brilliant men to have existed but Marx is also wrong? Why do we accept the theories of all other brilliant men except Marx? His idea is contradictory. He also gets many things wrong in his lecture about Marx’ theories (probably because he studies philosophy not Marxism). For example:

Marx was a young Hegelian but he later fiercely rejected Hegel. 
Marx did not accept Hegel’s dialectic of thesis-antithesis-synthesis. Marx’ dialectic is about opposing but interdependent forces that create contradiction followed by resolution. This sounds similar but is indeed different.

Communism is the zenith of humanity, the utopian ideal. We have never experienced this in any country at anytime. Humans have practiced SOCIALISM, which is different and Marx did not write about. The communist party of the Soviet Union for example did not consider its members communists but as socialists. The west called them communists as a pejorative to show that their imperfect societies demonstrated the failure of communism. 
the professor doesn’t seem to understand that religion is a technology. 

Communism only created a lot of wars if you believe revolutions are negative things rather than progressive and natural parts of human history. The framing here is as if imperialist wars and revolution are equal things. They are in reality qualitatively different things. War is negative but not “bad” in and of itself. It’s like anger it’s normal and human and sometimes justified sometimes not. I guess it is the professors idea that war against poverty is the same as wars to impose poverty or that revolutions don’t exist unless the result is a utopia?

Capitalism is not a materialistic system in the philosophical sense. It is idealistic: the abstraction and universality of money, the idea that anyone who works hard will get rich, the poor have bad morals, the cult of science and on and on. Communists don’t believe equality will make everyone happy, they believe it will make everyone fed and have a roof and a job. Emotions don’t enter into the communist framework. It’s funny he talks about who is and isn’t brainwashed when he has the ideal western mindset. That he knows the truth (American cultural ideas) and the rest is “brainwashed”. Such an infantile word. 

Marx was not an aristocrat because his family was previously jewish (his mother and father converted to Christianity). His father was a lawyer which would make him middle-class. He later worked (obviously) as a journalist, author and devoted activist. He maintained a slightly lower standard of living than his family. 

Capitalism absorbed communist practices like public schooling and idea of which Marx was well aware of existing because public schooling had existed before he was born. Social programs can be enacted under capitalism and often are to quell revolution. A social democracy is not a socialist or communist society it is a capitalist one! This is the same attempt to equate communism and capitalism we saw from some of histories greatest monsters (early 20th century Germany, Italy and their proxies). The conclusions of the professors ideas are actually looking quite frightening. 

Marx did not believe history was linear! This is something anti-marxists cannot seem to agree upon. Marx’s conception of history is as a spiral moving outward.

A vanguard party is not a technocracy. Marx didn’t believe this nor did any Marxist Leninist parties or countries practice technocracy. The United States for example does, we are lead by lawyers, business men, and scientists. In the Soviet Union most politicians were former proletariat. 

He says China’s bureaucracy is uncreative and that people are complaining despite being fed. This is untrue 90% of people approve of the CPC. Almost no one is complaining.  

I do however agree with his thoughts on China being a better country during the cultural revolution and the North Korea is actually joyful place.  This is also true of Stalin era Soviet Union, GDR and other places. However I’m not sure if NK will outlive the south. ### **@MengzisDisciple** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgwgVlitu2fid73Jw0d4AaABAg)

The argument itself is flawed.  He is basing it on the Evangelical "prosperity gospel" BS.  The more money and wealth you have (the more selfish you are), this leads to constant conflict with others (anxiety).  It's how the mind works--selfish behavior activates the amygdala system (Fight/Flight center) which creates anxiety/fear (cortisol), but cortisol itself has dopamine attached (desires), but when these desires are fulfilled, dopamine itself activates cortisol (anxieties and fears).  This is Dark Path of Yoda (and Cicero).  It's what the Stoics try to control (as do the Confucians/Taoists/Buddhists).  It's how Neoliberals control the masses by feeding them both fears/anger (cortisol) and desires via advertising and propaganda, where greed is good (dopamine).    This is the VICE mechanism in the brain.  

Hegel and Kant's goal was to teach people to be able to control this VICE center and then activate their inner VIRTUE center (Idealism).  Which modern neuroscience has identified as the Insula system (where you find serotonin--peaceful joy, oxytocin--love/empathy, and noradrenaline--spiritual hallucinations and creativity)  In most cultures it is identified with the essence of God (GEIST) or Tian (Heaven in China--behind both Yin/Yang).  Wang Yang-ming and the Heart-Mind school which followed Mengzi (human nature is good---made up of four cardinal virtues, which are innate feelings) also teaches this philosophy (idealism...).

The error of Marx was he "turned Hegel on his head", which just means he went from Virtues as the highest goal, back to VICES as the highest goal (which actually correlates with modern Neoliberal society--Greed and Selfishness are the highest good).  Evangelicals and most religions also follow this--as they teach the reason for being good is selfish in nature, either being afraid of hell (cortisol activation) or being rewarded in heaven or on earth (dopamine activation).  Most Religions (including Communism) teach human nature is Evil.  This is just Materialism.  It's why most societies are currently controlled by evil Sith-Lords who only care about themselves (whether billionaires or politicians, or a mix of both).  

That a so-called Philosopher like the Professor here doesn't know this is just sad.  The motto of the University of Hong Kong is WISDOM and VIRTUE--the highest goals of TRUE PHILOSOPHERS.  And yes REASON plays a major role--as you must decide which side of your nature you are going to follow in any given situation (as both are based on emotions or feelings, emotions connected to external things).  Reason in this sense is the Force of Star Wars (a philosophy based on Taoism and Confucianism).  I give food to the poor--but if I give it all, I myself will starve, so it is about the MIDDLE PATH (the Doctrine of the Mean in Confucianism, or the I-Ching if you understand the symbols).

### **@arvindchander967**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=UgwdR83kn2JK-ddhftB4AaABAg)

While his understanding of Marx is not accurate, his rebuttal of the Marxist theory of historical materialism is correct. He didn't elucidate the critique really well. My understanding is the marxist form of materialism is focuses more on the world outside of oneself. It underestimates the material reality within humans themselves. The material reality of the human psyche is it has developed a mental simulation of the world within itself. Basically it has developed consciousness. And fundamentally the human consciousness is the world of appearance. Its not the thing in itself. This causes a split in the human psyche between living unconsciously(like non human animals) and living consciously. This causes anxiety because suddenly now you are aware of your own mortality. Non human animals don't have a sense of 'self' and live ephemerally, moment to moment unlike humans who have developed consciousness and are now able to retain memories and know they are going to die. This develops the fiction of 'self'. They are no longer able to perceive themselves as one with the universe and view themselves as separate from it. Its kind of obvious now why this would cause immense anxiety. Now you have to contend with your own morality and the meaning of your life. Non human animals dont have this mental theatre inside themselves. The moment they have any physiological impulse, they discharge it.

Therefore this existential anxeity naturally drives humans to religion. Primitive religions were shamanistic among the hunter gatherers. These shamans were rare individuals who have achieved the ability to turn off their consciousness and become one with reality itself. But these primitive religions were so varied and different. Why would they be different if the cause of the problem is the same amongst everybody? This is where the material reality of the outside world comes in. Tribes in different geographical locations came up with different types of religions. The material reality outside them was so varied. Some places were cold, some were hot, some places were highly forested, some places were an ocean of grassland. You get the point. This caused different types of religions. But the material reality of the psyche is the same amongst all. The material reality of the human brain makes us predisposed to religion and spirituality. But here is the kicker. These religions are ultimately still fictions. They were fictions that can only approximate reality. So Humanity is doomed to keep iterating different fictional models of reality but can never accurately describe it. And this is what drives humans history. As technologies progress, we start revising our religions. And communism is the latest iteration of this religious striving. 

My theory is the primitive religions were much better approximations than the later ones. They were able to incorporate all the physiological drives of the human body more than the later ones. The primitive religions(pagan religions) had gods for lust, love, food. Gods for natural elements like sky, sun, water, etc. Gods for war, underworld, etc. Basic point is they were able to capture the full range of human experience. Later religions vilified many natural impulses like war, lust, etc. Unlike popular marxist myth that hunter gatherer societies were some form of primitive communism, these societies were ones that drew immense pleasure from killing the enemy tribe and to prove the superiority of their own gods. Primitive communism did exist, but only within the tribe, but this does not extend outside the tribe. Now the material reality of why you would want to vanquish the other tribe all boils down to the selfish desire to provide a comfortable life for your progeny. This desire is maybe the one immutable human desire that cannot be done away with because of evolutionary pressures. And this is what communism suppresses. The selfish desire to accumulate resources for your progeny. In modern day, tribes have been replaced by races. The material reality of genetic difference cannot be overcome to form a globalized proletariat class. Race and religious differences are not false consciousness. Its the material reality that communism cannot overcome. The only way for communism to work will be an all out global war among races and religions and ultimately a sole winner, a genetically homogeneous  group that can inherit this world and implement communism. Sort of what Eren from Attack on Titan had in mind. And this is becoming increasingly obvious in our modern experiments of multiculturalism in the western world. Immigration is now the single major issue in Europe and America. And it's only going to get worse notwithstanding all the secular people who don't see 'race'. In times of crises one will stick with their own race even if they are extremely liberal and rational. Ultimately you don't want anything happening to your extended family and interpolating that, your race. This is why despite the varied political opinions of Israelis, most of them still don't care about what's happening to the Palestinians. Even if rationally you know what's going on is wrong, you cannot empathize with their plight on a human level because you know they harbour ill intent(justified or unjustified) toward you. They know that in the hypothetical situation of roles being reversed and if the Palestinians had superior technological and military might, they would not take the high road and would inflict the same suffering on them.

Sorry for rambling on and going on multiple side tangents. My basic point is Marxism is lousy materialism. The meme 'Muh human nature' is infact true and valid criticism of communism.
