### **@andredias5284** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=Ugw263Yy6MAo0v_ZKcV4AaABAg)

This isn't correct. He mixes Marx with Marxism-Leninism and ignores multiple concepts by Marx. Marx actually was right into looking at capitalism and how capital did accumulate too quickly into the power of too few people while living conditions for everyone else were getting worse than in previous times. What Marx said is that this system wouldn't be sustainable in the long run and that would create crisis. These crisis would allow the workers to take over and eliminate the middle men and those who did concentrate too much wealth, which is genius even by a self interested pov rather than just plain solidarity.
Indeeed, what Marx did see as structural flaws into that system did lead to crisis and capitalism as he knew it did die. We no longer have really liberal capitalism in the sense of capitalism running rampant without state control nor worker's bodies. Union busting today looks like a child's play if compared to how things were during those times.
What maybe Marx didn't anticipate was that capitalism in the core countries of the system (where he did predict the revolution would take place) would absorb the leftist agenda while making it sterile and something that would strengthen the system. Worker's rights now doesn't aim towards overthrowing the system or getting autonomy; social democracy or eurocommunism did eliminate much of the communist appeal; the revolution was commodified and became part of a personal identity rather than a collective, class strugle.
Btw, Marx didn't mean that capitalism would fall soon or inevitably. Rather he did say that as all systems one day it would fall, but only if the proletariat was able to organize and overthrow it. He did point out that trying to reform it (utopic socialism) would just keep capitalism afloat, which it did (and more efficiently than he did think, thus the reason one could say he might have made a mistake predicting that the revolutions would take part into the richest portions of the system). It was rather into underdeveloped countries, the system's weakest links that the revolution did take place - and mostly made by peasants rather than a political counscious group of industrial, urban workers. So Marxism-Leninism was likely the real application of Marx's ideas.

I am glad you pointed out, "The liberation of the working class has to be the act of the workers themselves." The American socialist Daniel De Leon had that quote in mind when he came up with the socialist industrial union program. He quoted Marx in saying that the working class had to organize into AN organization of labor.  The socialist industrial union program would unite all industries and trades under a central authority of the rank and file of the union. We see that with the Industrial Workers of the World, which De Leon co founded, but unfortunately the anachro-syndicalists took over the IWW. They threw out the political action from the preamble but retained direct action. The IWW believe that the only course to defeat capitalism is through direct action. However, the IWW does not interfere with the political beliefs of their members. That was good enough for me to become a member since it is an anti capitalist union.

### **@12labours59**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m24LnQ-Ksjk&lc=Ugy-g22yI_py8eEkBml4AaABAg)

What Marx actually got wrong? Holistically? He underestimated the role of cognitive limits and overestimated moral scalability. Dunbars number... 

Communism and Socialism do not scale beyond a tribe. Marxist systems implicitly rely on shared moral commitment, alignment of incentives with the collective good. All of those collapse when group size exceeds cognitive limits of Dunbars number (~150 people). 

Beyond that scale trust is replaced with bureaucracy, surveillance, or coercion, time and time again. 

Dunbars number doesn’t refute Marx, but it does expose a hidden assumption Marx never solved. He may have understood some measure of class dynamics, but he did not understand human cognitive constraints. 

And also… when you dig into the life of Marx, I’m just not taking economic advice from a man that wouldn’t pay his own bills.🤷‍♂️

This is the worst of his lectures that I've seen.  Sad, and I had hoped that he used references.  But I wonder if most of the way he is talking is actually playing a devil's advocate.  A lot of what he says is factually and economically wrong -- measurable levels of equality in living standards are rising faster under capitalism than any other system - and he says capitalism needs to expand... the reason it expands is because return on investment is highest when you have smart people who live in mud huts and organize them to create a copy of modern civilization, which is what happened with western investment in China.  The expansion of Capital reduces the inequality of the newly added countries.  If america had kept it's capital, if europe had kept it's capital, the east would still be isolated and unable to replicate the institutions necessary for development.  The pursuit of return on investment is underpinned by the desire to benefit those whom you employ.  It's a mutual agreement for mutual benefit.  Slavery and exploitation are more common in dictator-lead autocracies, and in backwards countries, in parts of africa and undeveloped areas of asia.
